Figure 5: Left: Frozen Paradox, frozen RR-MoA beats full FT by 12–79%. Right: Observation 1
validated (ρ=−0.88, p<0.002). Traffic and Solar are boundary cases.


5            Conclusion and Limitations
MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail for structural reasons: the normalization needed
for distribution shift also strips the per-window statistics a downstream router needs. Raw pre-
normalization routing fixes the failure and motivates Residual-IA+ ; R(D) identifies where it applies.
The broader pattern: when a foundation model’s input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router
needs, optimization-side rescues (load balancing, z-loss, ReLU routing) cannot recover what is
structurally absent — the fix is restoring the missing input, not retuning the optimizer. The same
mechanism appears in a vision control (Appendix H.1); generalization to untested normalization
families is bounded by Observation 1.

Limitations. (i) Normalization specificity: our diagnosis applies to instance-level normalization
(RevIN, BatchNorm, GroupNorm); attention-based or learned normalizers are uncharacterized.
(ii) Signal-ratio boundary cases: R(D) is a single monotone proxy and is not sharp on datasets
with strong shape-statistics coupling (Solar, R=0.06). (iii) Tasks: forecasting and imputation only;
anomaly detection, classification, and irregularly-sampled tasks are deferred.

References
 [1] Ashish Vaswani, Noam Shazeer, Niki Parmar, Jakob Uszkoreit, Llion Jones, Aidan N. Gomez,
     Lukasz Kaiser, and Illia Polosukhin. Attention is all you need. In Advances in Neural Informa-
     tion Processing Systems (NeurIPS), volume 30, 2017.
 [2] Rishi Bommasani, Drew A. Hudson, Ehsan Adeli, Russ Altman, Simran Arora, Sydney von Arx,
     et al. On the opportunities and risks of foundation models. arXiv preprint arXiv:2108.07258,
     2021.
 [3] Jacob Devlin, Ming-Wei Chang, Kenton Lee, and Kristina Toutanova. BERT: Pre-training of
     deep bidirectional transformers for language understanding. In Proceedings of the Conference
     of the North American Chapter of the Association for Computational Linguistics (NAACL),
     pages 4171–4186, 2019.
 [4] Tom B. Brown, Benjamin Mann, Nick Ryder, Melanie Subbiah, Jared Kaplan, Prafulla Dhariwal,
     Arvind Neelakantan, Pranav Shyam, Girish Sastry, Amanda Askell, et al. Language models are
     few-shot learners. In Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS), volume 33,
     pages 1877–1901, 2020.
 [5] Alexey Dosovitskiy, Lucas Beyer, Alexander Kolesnikov, Dirk Weissenborn, Xiaohua Zhai,
     Thomas Unterthiner, Mostafa Dehghani, Matthias Minderer, Georg Heigold, Sylvain Gelly,
     Jakob Uszkoreit, and Neil Houlsby. An image is worth 16x16 words: Transformers for image
     recognition at scale. In International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR), 2021.
 [6] Mononito Goswami, Konrad Szafer, Arjun Choudhry, Yifu Cai, Shuo Li, and Artur Dubrawski.
     MOMENT: A family of open time-series foundation models. In Proceedings of the 41st
     International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML), volume 235 of PMLR, pages 16115–
     16152, 2024.

```
     (i) Exact decomposition. I(X; E) − I(S; E) = I(M, Σ; E | S) ≥ 0.
    (ii) Quantitative bound. Without any independence assumption,

                           I(X; E) − I(S; E) ≥ I(M, Σ; E) − I(M, Σ; S),

         where ε := I(M, Σ; S) ≥ 0 captures any residual “leakage” of the stripped statistics
         through the shape. Equality holds iff (M, Σ) ⊥⊥ S | E.

In the corner case where routing signal lives entirely in the stripped statistics (E a deterministic
function of (M, Σ) and (M, Σ) ⊥       ⊥ S; Part (iii), Appendix D), I(X; E) − I(S; E) = H(E):
RevIN destroys the entire routing distribution. We therefore use the signal ratio R(D) =
[Var(M ) + Var(Σ)]/Var(S) as a tractable monotone proxy. The bound is empirically tight (CCA
ε ∈ [0.09, 1.26] nats, KSG-cross-validated within ±0.26 [25]; classifier retains only 16–41% of
routing entropy, gap 0.075 nats; Appendix L); learnable α ∈ [0, 1] converges to α < 0.5 on all
six datasets (30/30; Appendix K). R(D) predicts the raw-routing benefit at Spearman ρ=−0.88
(p<0.002, n=9; Figure 5), whereas H(E | S) alone does not (ρ=−0.12): routing potential in the
stripped statistics matters, not MI loss. Traffic (R=0.14, ∆=+2.9%) is correctly flagged a boundary
case; Solar (R=0.06) is the one outlier (SSR +43%; Appendix F).
Cross-backbone, task and horizon generalization. RR-MoA wins all 21 MOMENT-small/large
configurations (26–79%; Table N.1), 18/18 Moirai (3–31%), and 6/6 Moirai-MoE [12] datasets
(37–90%; Table N.2): adapter-level and backbone-internal routing operate on complementary axes.
The negative controls predicted by Observation 1 (Chronos and Timer-XL [10], neither using instance
normalization) show no collapse and no RR-MoA gain (4/18 within chance; Appendix N). Top-2
sparse routing retains most of the dense gain at a fraction of the active-expert FLOPs (Appendix I);
imputation (7/8; Appendix J) and horizons H∈{96,192,336,720} (24/24; Table C.1) confirm ro-
bustness. Residual-IA+ (Appendix P.1) extends to all 6 datasets across 5 backbones and 4 horizons
(Table P.7).

652	{\looseness=-1 \textbf{Cross-backbone, task, horizon.}\label{sec:cross_backbone}\label{sec:topk} RR-MoA wins 21/21 MOMENT-small/large ($26$--$79\%$; Table~\ref{tab:cross_backbone}), 18/18 Moirai ($3$--$31\%$), and 6/6 Moirai-MoE~\citep{liu2025moiraimoe} ($37$--$90\%$; Table~\ref{tab:moirai_moe}) — adapter- and backbone-internal routing are complementary. Negative controls predicted by Observation~\ref{thm:mi_decomp} (Chronos, Timer-XL~\citep{liu2025timerxl}, no instance norm) show no collapse and no gain (4/18 within chance; Appendix~\ref{app:cross_backbone}). Top-2 sparse routing retains most of the dense gain at lower FLOPs (Appendix~\ref{app:topk}); imputation $7/8$ (Appendix~\ref{app:imputation}) and horizons $H{\in}\{96,192,336,720\}$ $24/24$ (Table~\ref{tab:horizon}) confirm robustness; \textbf{Residual-IA\textsuperscript{+}} extends to 6 datasets $\times$ 5 backbones $\times$ 4 horizons (Appendix~\ref{app:gate_pathology}, Table~\ref{tab:residual_ia_plus_mhcb}).}
```

5            Conclusion and Limitations
MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail for structural reasons: the normalization needed
for distribution shift also strips the per-window statistics a downstream router needs. Raw pre-
normalization routing fixes the failure and motivates Residual-IA+ ; R(D) identifies where it applies.
The broader pattern: when a foundation model’s input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router
needs, optimization-side rescues (load balancing, z-loss, ReLU routing) cannot recover what is
structurally absent — the fix is restoring the missing input, not retuning the optimizer. The same
mechanism appears in a vision control (Appendix H.1); generalization to untested normalization
families is bounded by Observation 1.

Limitations. (i) Normalization specificity: our diagnosis applies to instance-level normalization
(RevIN, BatchNorm, GroupNorm); attention-based or learned normalizers are uncharacterized.
(ii) Signal-ratio boundary cases: R(D) is a single monotone proxy and is not sharp on datasets
with strong shape-statistics coupling (Solar, R=0.06). (iii) Tasks: forecasting and imputation only;
anomaly detection, classification, and irregularly-sampled tasks are deferred.


References
 [1] Ashish Vaswani, Noam Shazeer, Niki Parmar, Jakob Uszkoreit, Llion Jones, Aidan N. Gomez,
     Lukasz Kaiser, and Illia Polosukhin. Attention is all you need. In Advances in Neural Informa-
     tion Processing Systems (NeurIPS), volume 30, 2017.

 [2] Rishi Bommasani, Drew A. Hudson, Ehsan Adeli, Russ Altman, Simran Arora, Sydney von Arx,
     et al. On the opportunities and risks of foundation models. arXiv preprint arXiv:2108.07258,
     2021.

 [3] Jacob Devlin, Ming-Wei Chang, Kenton Lee, and Kristina Toutanova. BERT: Pre-training of
     deep bidirectional transformers for language understanding. In Proceedings of the Conference
     of the North American Chapter of the Association for Computational Linguistics (NAACL),
     pages 4171–4186, 2019.

 [4] Tom B. Brown, Benjamin Mann, Nick Ryder, Melanie Subbiah, Jared Kaplan, Prafulla Dhariwal,
     Arvind Neelakantan, Pranav Shyam, Girish Sastry, Amanda Askell, et al. Language models are
     few-shot learners. In Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS), volume 33,
     pages 1877–1901, 2020.

 [5] Alexey Dosovitskiy, Lucas Beyer, Alexander Kolesnikov, Dirk Weissenborn, Xiaohua Zhai,
     Thomas Unterthiner, Mostafa Dehghani, Matthias Minderer, Georg Heigold, Sylvain Gelly,
     Jakob Uszkoreit, and Neil Houlsby. An image is worth 16x16 words: Transformers for image
     recognition at scale. In International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR), 2021.

In the corner case where routing signal lives entirely in the stripped statistics (E a deterministic
function of (M, Σ) and (M, Σ) ⊥       ⊥ S; Part (iii), Appendix D), I(X; E) − I(S; E) = H(E):
RevIN destroys the entire routing distribution. We therefore use the signal ratio R(D) =
[Var(M ) + Var(Σ)]/Var(S) as a tractable monotone proxy. The bound is empirically tight (CCA
ε ∈ [0.09, 1.26] nats, KSG-cross-validated within ±0.26 [25]; classifier retains only 16–41% of
routing entropy, gap 0.075 nats; Appendix L); learnable α ∈ [0, 1] converges to α < 0.5 on all
six datasets (30/30; Appendix K). R(D) predicts the raw-routing benefit at Spearman ρ=−0.88
(p<0.002, n=9; Figure 5), whereas H(E | S) alone does not (ρ=−0.12): routing potential in the
stripped statistics matters, not MI loss. Traffic (R=0.14, ∆=+2.9%) is correctly flagged a boundary
case; Solar (R=0.06) is the one outlier (SSR +43%; Appendix F).
Cross-backbone, task, horizon. RR-MoA wins 21/21 MOMENT-small/large (26–79%; Table N.1),
18/18 Moirai (3–31%), and 6/6 Moirai-MoE [12] (37–90%; Table N.2) — adapter- and backbone-
internal routing are complementary. Negative controls predicted by Observation 1 (Chronos, Timer-
XL [10], no instance norm) show no collapse and no gain (4/18 within chance; Appendix N).
Top-2 sparse routing retains most of the dense gain at lower FLOPs (Appendix I); imputation 7/8
(Appendix J) and horizons H∈{96, 192, 336, 720} 24/24 (Table C.1) confirm robustness; Residual-

57	\begin{abstract}
58	A common time series foundation model (TSFM) recipe pairs a shared, frozen backbone with a task-specific head. Because downstream series mix heterogeneous regimes that benefit from different aggregation topologies, the natural upgrade is a mixture-of-experts (MoE) head: a router over architecturally distinct experts, one selected per input window. We identify a failure mode of this upgrade that arises with instance normalization. Once any backbone layer is unfrozen, the router converges to a one-hot assignment, routing entropy collapses to $0.000$, and only one expert ever receives a gradient update. We characterize and name this failure mode \emph{normalization-induced routing collapse}. The mechanism is direct: instance-normalization layers (e.g., RevIN) strip the per-window mean and variance the router needs. A $720$-run sweep across standard MoE rescue mechanisms (load balancing, z-loss, entropy regularization, ReLU routing, expert-choice) converges to the same collapsed state, recovering at most $10.9\%$ MSE. This remains $2.7\times$ worse than our causal intervention, because the failure is in the router's input, not its optimization. We formalize the mechanism via a mutual-information decomposition. The resulting signal-ratio statistic, computable before training, predicts dataset vulnerability to collapse (Spearman $\rho{=}{-}0.88$, $p{<}0.002$) and correctly flags boundary cases. The diagnosis prescribes a one-line \emph{causal intervention}: \emph{Raw-Routed Mixture of Adapters} (RR-MoA) routes on the raw, pre-normalization input. RR-MoA sets a new SOTA for adapter-based TSFM transfer under a strictly frozen-backbone protocol: it wins all $54/54$ configurations on the primary backbone (6 datasets $\times$ 3 freeze levels $\times$ 3 seeds, $26$--$79\%$ MSE improvement over the best fixed adapter), strictly beats LoRA, TRACE, AdaMix, and full fine-tuning at $p{<}0.001$, and generalizes across four further backbones and an imputation task. Eight causal controls including a vision-modality replication isolate the cause to instance normalization, and the diagnosis predicts a \emph{Frozen Paradox}: frozen RR-MoA beats full fine-tuning by $12$--$79\%$ — a two-factor gap dominated by the MoE pool's architectural advantage ($26$--$79\%$ at matched freeze level), with a smaller within-architecture freeze effect ($\leq 13\%$, frozen best on $4/6$) causally attributable to gradient co-adaptation (Proposition~\ref{prop:frozen} + RDGF + RevIN-disabled full-FT). Applying the same raw-signal principle at the expert level (Residual-IA\textsuperscript{+}) closes the gap on $5/6$ LTSF datasets at $H{=}96$ ($107/123$ cells across six backbones $\times$ four horizons).
59	\end{abstract}
```

552	
553	{\looseness=-1 \textbf{Rawness vs.\ bypass and dose-response.} Replacing the router's raw input with RevIN-normalized input degrades MSE by $60$--$88\%$ while entropy stays high (Table~\ref{tab:router_input}, Appendix~\ref{app:routing_ablations}), confirming the router relies on the \emph{content} of the stripped statistics, not merely the bypass path. A dose-response interpolation $\mathbf{x}_\alpha = (1{-}\alpha)\,\mathbf{x}_\text{raw} + \alpha\,\text{RevIN}(\mathbf{x}_\text{raw})$ reveals a \emph{phase transition} (Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}; 150 runs): MSE degrades by $+5.4\%$ for $\alpha{\leq}0.75$ but jumps to $+283\%$ at $\alpha{=}1.0$, while entropy stays in $[1.43, 1.57]$: normalization renders routing uninformative without reducing diversity. The phase transition threshold is quantitatively predicted at $\alpha^* = R(\mathcal{D})/(R(\mathcal{D})+1) \approx 0.40$ for the median benchmark (Appendix~\ref{app:prop2_depth}). A temporal-shuffle ablation confirms routing depends on $(M,\Sigma)$ statistics, not temporal ordering (Appendix~\ref{app:routing_ablations}).}
554	
555	
556	\textbf{Why RevIN destroys routing: formal analysis.} The experiments above converge on a single mechanism: instance normalization strips the per-window statistics that the router uses to tell input regimes apart. To pin this down, we ask what information the router actually has access to before and after normalization. Each input window $\mathbf{x}_i$ decomposes into a mean $M_i = \mu(\mathbf{x}_i)$, a scale $\Sigma_i = \sigma(\mathbf{x}_i)$, and a shape $\mathbf{S}_i = (\mathbf{x}_i - M_i)/\Sigma_i$. RevIN maps $\mathbf{x}_i \mapsto \mathbf{S}_i$ and discards $(M_i, \Sigma_i)$, so the question reduces to how much routing information lives in the discarded part. The mutual-information chain rule gives a clean answer.
557	
558	\begin{observation}[Routing Information Loss Under Instance Normalization]
559	\label{thm:mi_decomp}
560	Let $X$ be a random input window, $E \in \{1,\ldots,K\}$ the expert assignment produced by any deterministic router, and $S = (X - M)/\Sigma$ the RevIN-normalized shape.
561	\begin{enumerate}
562	    \item[\textup{(i)}] \textbf{Exact decomposition.} $I(X; E) - I(S; E) = I(M, \Sigma; E \mid S) \geq 0$.
563	    \item[\textup{(ii)}] \textbf{Quantitative bound.} Without any independence assumption,

routing are complementary. Predicted negative controls (Chronos, Timer-XL [10], no instance norm)
show no collapse and no gain (4/18 within chance; Appendix N). Top-2 routing retains most of the
dense gain (Appendix I); imputation 7/8 (Appendix J) and horizons H∈{96, 192, 336, 720} 24/24
(Table C.1) confirm robustness; Residual-IA+ extends to 6 datasets × 5 backbones × 4 horizons
(Appendix P.1).
5   Conclusion and Limitations
MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail for structural reasons: the normalization needed
for distribution shift also strips the per-window statistics a downstream router needs. Raw pre-
normalization routing fixes the failure and motivates Residual-IA+ ; R(D) identifies where it applies.
The broader pattern: when a foundation model’s input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router
needs, optimization-side rescues (load balancing, z-loss, ReLU routing) cannot recover what is
structurally absent — the fix is restoring the missing input, not retuning the optimizer. The same
mechanism appears in a vision control (Appendix H.1); generalization to untested normalization
families is bounded by Observation 1.

Limitations. (i) Normalization specificity: our diagnosis applies to instance-level normalization
(RevIN, BatchNorm, GroupNorm); attention-based or learned normalizers are uncharacterized.
(ii) Signal-ratio boundary cases: R(D) is a single monotone proxy and is not sharp on datasets
with strong shape-statistics coupling (Solar, R=0.06). (iii) Tasks: forecasting and imputation only;
anomaly detection, classification, and irregularly-sampled tasks are deferred.

References
 [1] Ashish Vaswani, Noam Shazeer, Niki Parmar, Jakob Uszkoreit, Llion Jones, Aidan N. Gomez,
     Lukasz Kaiser, and Illia Polosukhin. Attention is all you need. In Advances in Neural Informa-
     tion Processing Systems (NeurIPS), volume 30, 2017.

full FT unfreezes all 8 blocks. Wilcoxon, Bonferroni-corrected. Grey rows: from-scratch calibration
anchors.

     Best LoRA (108-run sweep)                 1.154 ± 0.000            0.956 ± 0.072          0.600 ± 0.052       <0.001∗∗∗

     RR-MoA (Top-2, frozen)                    0.680 ± 0.027            0.564 ± 0.059         0.276 ± 0.017             —

RR-MoA outperforms every baseline (p<0.001). The independent ensemble (same experts, no router)
is 36–46% worse, confirming learned routing adds value beyond diversity.


                                                                    8
Closing the DLinear gap. DLinear (49K params, from scratch) outperforms frozen adapters in
absolute MSE (Table 4, grey rows). The gap reflects information destruction by the normalization-
encoding pipeline (k-NN and Ridge diagnostics in Appendix M), not adapter capacity. As the
diagnosis predicts, restoring raw-signal access at the expert level closes this gap: Residual-IA+
(a dual-stream architecture in which a shared NLinear raw-input branch is added to each expert’s
output; Appendix P.1, Figure P.1) matches or beats DLinear on 6/6 datasets at H=96 (up to −7.5%),
generalizing to 107/123 cells across six backbones. A self-routed variant SR-RIA+ (Appendix P.2)
achieves the same 6/6 match-or-beat with 3 outright wins; a TSFM-free Pure Raw-MLP MoE control
(Appendix P.3) localizes the TSFM’s contribution and rules out the alternative hypothesis that the
raw branch alone is doing all the work.
Rawness vs. bypass and dose-response. Replacing the router’s raw input with RevIN-normalized
input degrades MSE by 60–88% while entropy stays high (Table F.1): the router relies on the
content of the stripped statistics, not the bypass path. A dose-response interpolation xα =
(1−α)xraw + α RevIN(xraw ) reveals a phase transition (Figure D.1; 150 runs): MSE degrades by
+5.4% for α≤0.75 but jumps to +283% at α=1, with entropy held in [1.43, 1.57] throughout. The
threshold is quantitatively predicted at α∗ = R(D)/(R(D) + 1) ≈ 0.40 for the median benchmark

551	\textbf{Closing the DLinear gap.} DLinear (49K params, from scratch) outperforms frozen adapters in absolute MSE (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}, grey rows). The gap reflects information destruction by the normalization-encoding pipeline (k-NN and Ridge diagnostics in Appendix~\ref{app:diagnostic}), not adapter capacity. As the diagnosis predicts, restoring raw-signal access \emph{at the expert level} closes this gap: \textbf{Residual-IA\textsuperscript{+}} (a dual-stream architecture in which a shared NLinear raw-input branch is added to each expert's output; Appendix~\ref{app:gate_pathology}, Figure~\ref{fig:residual_ia_arch}) matches or beats DLinear on \textbf{6/6 datasets} at $H{=}96$ (up to $-7.5\%$), generalizing to $107/123$ cells across six backbones. A self-routed variant SR-RIA\textsuperscript{+} (Appendix~\ref{app:sr_ria}) achieves the same 6/6 match-or-beat with $3$ outright wins; a TSFM-free Pure Raw-MLP MoE control (Appendix~\ref{app:raw_mlp_moe}) localizes the TSFM's contribution and rules out the alternative hypothesis that the raw branch alone is doing all the work.
552	
553	{\looseness=-1 \textbf{Rawness vs.\ bypass and dose-response.} Replacing the router's raw input with RevIN-normalized input degrades MSE by $60$--$88\%$ while entropy stays high (Table~\ref{tab:router_input}): the router relies on the \emph{content} of the stripped statistics, not the bypass path. A dose-response interpolation $\mathbf{x}_\alpha = (1{-}\alpha)\mathbf{x}_\text{raw} + \alpha\,\text{RevIN}(\mathbf{x}_\text{raw})$ reveals a phase transition (Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}; $150$ runs): MSE degrades by $+5.4\%$ for $\alpha{\leq}0.75$ but jumps to $+283\%$ at $\alpha{=}1$, with entropy held in $[1.43, 1.57]$ throughout. The threshold is quantitatively predicted at $\alpha^* = R(\mathcal{D})/(R(\mathcal{D})+1) \approx 0.40$ for the median benchmark (Appendix~\ref{app:prop2_depth}); a temporal-shuffle ablation confirms routing depends on $(M,\Sigma)$, not temporal ordering (Appendix~\ref{app:routing_ablations}).}
```

5   Conclusion and Limitations
MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail for structural reasons: the normalization needed
for distribution shift also strips the per-window statistics a downstream router needs. Raw pre-
normalization routing fixes the failure and motivates Residual-IA+ ; R(D) identifies where it applies.
The broader pattern: when a foundation model’s input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router
needs, optimization-side rescues cannot recover what is structurally absent — the fix is restoring the
input, not retuning the optimizer; the same mechanism appears in a vision control (Appendix H.1).
Limitations. (i) our diagnosis covers instance-level normalization (RevIN, BatchNorm, GroupNorm);
attention-based or learned normalizers are uncharacterized. (ii) R(D) is a single monotone proxy and
is not sharp on datasets with strong shape-statistics coupling (Solar, R=0.06). (iii) forecasting and
imputation only; anomaly detection, classification, and irregularly-sampled tasks are deferred.

References
 [1] Ashish Vaswani, Noam Shazeer, Niki Parmar, Jakob Uszkoreit, Llion Jones, Aidan N. Gomez,
     Lukasz Kaiser, and Illia Polosukhin. Attention is all you need. In Advances in Neural Informa-
     tion Processing Systems (NeurIPS), volume 30, 2017.

 [2] Rishi Bommasani, Drew A. Hudson, Ehsan Adeli, Russ Altman, Simran Arora, Sydney von Arx,
     et al. On the opportunities and risks of foundation models. arXiv preprint arXiv:2108.07258,
     2021.

5   Conclusion and Limitations
MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail because the normalization needed for distribution
shift also strips the per-window statistics the router needs; raw pre-normalization routing fixes
the failure, motivates Residual-IA+ , and R(D) identifies where it applies. More broadly: when a

the input rather than retuning the optimizer (replicated in a vision control, Appendix H.1). Limits:

uncharacterized); (ii) R(D) is a single monotone proxy, not sharp on shape-statistics-coupled data
(Solar, R=0.06); (iii) forecasting and imputation only.

absolute MSE (Table 4, grey rows). The gap reflects information destruction by the normalization-
encoding pipeline (k-NN/Ridge diagnostics, Appendix M), not adapter capacity. Restoring raw-signal
access at the expert level closes it: Residual-IA+ (shared NLinear raw branch + each expert’s gated
backbone residual; Appendix P.1) matches or beats DLinear on 6/6 datasets at H=96 (up to −7.5%),
and on 107/123 cells across six backbones. A self-routed SR-RIA+ (Appendix P.2) hits the same
6/6 with 3 outright wins; a TSFM-free Pure Raw-MLP MoE control (Appendix P.3) localizes the
TSFM’s contribution and rules out the raw branch acting alone.

657	\vspace{-0.4em}
658	\paragraph{Conclusion and limitations.} MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail because the normalization needed for distribution shift also strips the per-window statistics the router needs; raw pre-normalization routing fixes the failure, motivates Residual-IA\textsuperscript{+}, and $R(\mathcal{D})$ identifies where it applies. More broadly: when a foundation model's input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router needs, the fix is restoring the input rather than retuning the optimizer (replicated in a vision control, Appendix~\ref{app:vision_moe}). \textbf{Limits:} \emph{(i)}~our diagnosis covers instance-level normalization only (attention-based and learned normalizers uncharacterized); \emph{(ii)}~$R(\mathcal{D})$ is a single monotone proxy, not sharp on shape-statistics-coupled data (Solar, $R{=}0.06$); \emph{(iii)}~forecasting and imputation only.\label{page:end_main}

```
        Raw-Routed Mixture of Adapters: A Causal
      Intervention for Routing Collapse in Time Series
                     Foundation Models


       Anonymous Author 1                  Anonymous Author 2                Anonymous Author 3
       Anonymous Institution               Anonymous Institution             Anonymous Institution
      author1@example.com                 author2@example.com               author3@example.com



                                                 Abstract

            A common time series foundation model (TSFM) recipe pairs a shared frozen
            backbone with a task-specific head. Because downstream series mix heterogeneous
            regimes that benefit from different aggregation topologies, the natural upgrade is
            a mixture-of-experts (MoE) head — a router over architecturally distinct experts,
            one per input window. We identify a failure mode of this upgrade that arises with
            instance normalization: once any backbone layer is unfrozen, the router converges
            to a one-hot assignment, routing entropy collapses to 0.000, and only one expert
            ever receives a gradient. We name this normalization-induced routing collapse:
            instance-normalization layers (e.g., RevIN) strip the per-window mean and variance
            the router needs. A 720-run sweep across standard MoE rescue mechanisms (load
            balancing, z-loss, entropy regularization, ReLU routing, expert-choice) converges
            to the same collapsed state, recovering at most 10.9% MSE — 2.7× worse than our
            causal intervention, because the failure is in the router’s input, not its optimization.
            We formalize this via a mutual-information decomposition; the resulting signal-
            ratio statistic, computable before training, predicts dataset vulnerability (Spearman
            ρ=−0.88, p<0.002) and correctly flags boundary cases. The diagnosis prescribes
            a one-line fix: Raw-Routed Mixture of Adapters (RR-MoA) routes on the raw,
            pre-normalization input. RR-MoA sets a new SOTA for adapter-based TSFM
            transfer under a strictly frozen backbone: 54/54 wins on the primary backbone
            (26–79% MSE improvement over the best fixed adapter), strictly beats LoRA,
            TRACE, AdaMix, and full fine-tuning at p<0.001, and generalizes across four
            further backbones and an imputation task. Eight causal controls including a vision-
            modality replication isolate the cause to instance normalization, and predict a
            Frozen Paradox: frozen RR-MoA beats full fine-tuning by 12–79%, dominated
            by the MoE pool’s architectural advantage (26–79% at matched freeze level) with
            a smaller within-architecture freeze effect (≤ 13%) attributable to gradient co-
            adaptation (Proposition 1). Applying the same raw-signal principle at the expert
            level (Residual-IA+ ) closes the gap with DLinear on 107/123 cells across six
            backbones × four horizons.


1    Introduction
```

shared NLinear raw branch + gated backbone residual, closing the DLinear gap. Table 3 summarizes
all eight controls.

Table 3: Eight causal controls. Each rules out a competing explanation; the only mechanism
consistent with all 8 is that instance normalization strips the per-window (µ, σ) signal the router
needs.
#     Control                                Hypothesis (rejected)             Observed result ⇒ verdict
C1    RevIN ablation                         Collapse is intrinsic to MoE      Entropy 0.000 → 0.66–1.32 when RevIN disabled
                                             itself, not caused by             (Table 2) ⇒ RevIN is necessary for collapse
                                             normalization
C2    MOMENT vs Timer-XL                     Architecture, not                 Opposite trajectories with identical code, only norm
                                             normalization, causes             differs (Fig. 4) ⇒ normalization is sufficient
                                             collapse
C3    BatchNorm1d / GroupNorm                Collapse is unique to RevIN;      All three per-window normalizers collapse identically
                                             other per-window                  (App. H) ⇒ any norm that strips (µ, σ) triggers
                                             normalizers are immune            collapse
C4    5-family rescue sweep                  Optimization problem              0/12 configs recover; best is 2.7× worse than
                                             (load-balance, z-loss, ReLU,      RR-MoA (Table G.3) ⇒ not in the optimizer
                                             expert-choice)
C5    AdaMix with raw router                 Router architecture is the       Same router, swap input only: entropy → 1.41–1.57;
                                             bottleneck                       MSE −41–88% (App. G.2) ⇒ the input matters, not
                                                                              the router
C6    SR-MoA raw vs hidden routing           The fix only works for           Different router (per-expert sigmoid); hidden-routed
                                             RR-MoA’s specific Conv1d         +87–768% worse (Table F.2) ⇒ raw-input principle
                                             router design                    generalizes
C7    Vision ViT + InstanceNorm              Collapse only happens in         ResNet/ViT MoE on CIFAR-10 collapses identically
                                             time series, not other           (App. H.1) ⇒ mechanism is modality-general
                                             modalities
C8    (µ, σ) re-injection                    Just appending stripped stats     Re-injected hidden states still +42–239% worse,
                                             back fixes it                     11/18 still collapsed (App. F.1) ⇒ only raw
                                                                               pre-normalization input restores routing


Two distinct gains, one diagnosis. The mechanism decomposes into: (Q1) the best fixed adapter
on the same RevIN-normalized hidden states still loses to RR-MoA by 26–79% (Table 1), so
residual shape heterogeneity survives normalization and rewards diverse experts; (Q2) the signal-ratio
ρ=−0.88 measures dispatch-signal localization in the stripped statistics, which is why dense routing
also benefits from raw access and why Traffic (R=0.14) correctly does not improve. The Pure
Raw-MLP MoE ablation (Appendix P.3) shows the dataset-conditional choice between “MoE with
raw router” and “ensemble with raw input”: wherever instance normalization sits between input and
router, the routing signal must be re-injected from upstream.
Table 4: Baseline comparison (MOMENT-small, Top-2, 5 seeds, H=96). Above double rule: frozen;
full FT unfreezes all 8 blocks. Wilcoxon, Bonferroni-corrected. Grey rows: from-scratch calibration
anchors.

     Best LoRA (108-run sweep)                 1.154 ± 0.000            0.956 ± 0.072          0.600 ± 0.052       <0.001∗∗∗
     TRACE (frozen)                            1.195 ± 0.026            1.014 ± 0.073          0.457 ± 0.015       <0.001∗∗∗
     Ind. Ensemble (5 experts)                 1.081 ± 0.021            1.049 ± 0.032          0.509 ± 0.024       <0.001∗∗∗
     AdaMix (frozen)                           1.109 ± 0.031            1.008 ± 0.012          0.459 ± 0.017       <0.001∗∗∗

The principle is architecture-agnostic. The diagnosis predicts the same fix should work for any
router that reads raw input. We validate this with three router-level controls. SR-MoA [46–48]
(Self-Routed MoA, Figure F.1) drops the external router entirely: each expert has its own sigmoid
gate σk (Xraw ). SR-MoA outperforms RR-MoA by 13–42% across all 6 datasets (252 runs, six
backbones) and replicates the Frozen Paradox (frozen best on 4/6; Table F.2). Gating the same
SR-MoA architecture on hidden states degrades by +87–768% on all 6 datasets (p=0.016); on
Timer-XL (no RevIN) the same raw-vs-hidden comparison yields only +4–9% (15–100× smaller),
confirming the effect is normalization-induced. AdaMix-Raw (Appendix G.2) swaps AdaMix’s

the failure, motivates Residual-IA+ , and R(D) identifies where it applies. More broadly: when a
foundation model’s input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router needs, the fix is restoring
the input rather than retuning the optimizer (replicated in a vision control, Appendix H.1). Limits:
(i) our diagnosis covers instance-level normalization only (attention-based and learned normalizers
uncharacterized); (ii) R(D) is a single monotone proxy, not sharp on shape-statistics-coupled data
(Solar, R=0.06); (iii) forecasting and imputation only.

  "old_string": "A common time series foundation model (TSFM) recipe pairs a shared frozen backbone with a task-specific head. Because downstream series mix heterogeneous regimes that benefit from different aggregation topologies, the natural upgrade is a mixture-of-experts (MoE) head — a router over architecturally distinct experts, one per input window. We identify a failure mode of this upgrade that arises with instance normalization: once any backbone layer is unfrozen, the router converges to a one-hot assignment, routing entropy collapses to $0.000$, and only one expert ever receives a gradient. We name this \\emph{normalization-induced routing collapse}: instance-normalization layers (e.g., RevIN) strip the per-window mean and variance the router needs. A $720$-run sweep across standard MoE rescue mechanisms (load balancing, z-loss, entropy regularization, ReLU routing, expert-choice) converges to the same collapsed state, recovering at most $10.9\\%$ MSE — $2.7\\times$ worse than our causal intervention, because the failure is in the router's input, not its optimization. We formalize this via a mutual-information decomposition; the resulting signal-ratio statistic, computable before training, predicts dataset vulnerability (Spearman $\\rho{=}{-}0.88$, $p{<}0.002$) and correctly flags boundary cases. The diagnosis prescribes a one-line fix: \\emph{Raw-Routed Mixture of Adapters} (RR-MoA) routes on the raw, pre-normalization input. RR-MoA sets a new SOTA for adapter-based TSFM transfer under a strictly frozen backbone: $54/54$ wins on the primary backbone ($26$--$79\\%$ MSE improvement over the best fixed adapter), strictly beats LoRA, TRACE, AdaMix, and full fine-tuning at $p{<}0.001$, and generalizes across four further backbones and an imputation task. Eight causal controls including a vision-modality replication isolate the cause to instance normalization, and predict a \\emph{Frozen Paradox}: frozen RR-MoA beats full fine-tuning by $12$--$79\\%$, dominated by the MoE pool's architectural advantage ($26$--$79\\%$ at matched freeze level) with a smaller within-architecture freeze effect ($\\leq 13\\%$) attributable to gradient co-adaptation (Proposition~\\ref{prop:frozen}). Applying the same raw-signal principle at the expert level (Residual-IA\\textsuperscript{+}) closes the gap with DLinear on $107/123$ cells across six backbones $\\times$ four horizons.",
  "new_string": "The common time series foundation model (TSFM) recipe pairs a frozen backbone with a task-specific head. Because downstream series mix heterogeneous regimes, the natural upgrade is a mixture-of-experts (MoE) head — a router over architecturally distinct experts, one per window. We identify a failure mode of this upgrade under instance normalization: once any backbone layer is unfrozen, the router converges to a one-hot assignment, routing entropy collapses to $0.000$, and only one expert receives gradients. We name this \\emph{normalization-induced routing collapse}: instance-normalization layers (e.g., RevIN) strip the per-window mean and variance the router needs. A $720$-run sweep across standard MoE rescue mechanisms (load balancing, z-loss, entropy regularization, ReLU routing, expert-choice) recovers at most $10.9\\%$ MSE — $2.7\\times$ worse than our causal intervention, because the failure is in the router's input, not its optimization. We formalize this via a mutual-information decomposition; the resulting signal-ratio statistic, computable before training, predicts dataset vulnerability (Spearman $\\rho{=}{-}0.88$, $p{<}0.002$) and flags boundary cases. The fix is one line: \\emph{Raw-Routed Mixture of Adapters} (RR-MoA) routes on the raw, pre-normalization input. RR-MoA sets a new SOTA for adapter-based TSFM transfer under a strictly frozen backbone: $54/54$ wins on the primary backbone ($26$--$79\\%$ MSE improvement over the best fixed adapter), strictly beats LoRA, TRACE, AdaMix, and full fine-tuning at $p{<}0.001$, and generalizes across four further backbones and an imputation task. Eight causal controls including a vision-modality replication isolate the cause to instance normalization, and predict a \\emph{Frozen Paradox}: frozen RR-MoA beats full fine-tuning by $12$--$79\\%$, dominated by the MoE pool's architectural advantage with a smaller within-architecture freeze effect ($\\leq 13\\%$) attributable to gradient co-adaptation (Proposition~\\ref{prop:frozen}). Applying the same raw-signal principle at the expert level (Residual-IA\\textsuperscript{+}) closes the gap with DLinear on $107/123$ cells across six backbones $\\times$ four horizons."
}

Conclusion and limitations. MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail because the nor-
malization needed for distribution shift also strips the per-window statistics the router needs; raw
pre-normalization routing fixes it, motivates Residual-IA+ , and R(D) identifies where it applies. The
broader pattern: when a foundation model’s input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router needs,
the fix is restoring the input, not retuning the optimizer (replicated in a vision control, Appendix H.1).

(ii) R(D) is a single monotone proxy, not sharp on shape-statistics-coupled data (Solar, R=0.06);
(iii) forecasting and imputation only.

(Appendix P.1).

5            Conclusion and Limitations
MoE adapters on instance-normalized TSFMs fail because the normalization needed for distribution
shift also strips the per-window statistics the router needs; raw pre-normalization routing fixes it,
motivates Residual-IA+ , and R(D) identifies where it applies. The broader pattern: when a foundation
model’s input pipeline destroys signal a downstream router needs, the fix is restoring the input, not
retuning the optimizer (replicated in a vision control, Appendix H.1). Limits: (i) instance-level
normalization only (attention-based/learned normalizers uncharacterized); (ii) R(D) is a single
monotone proxy, not sharp on shape-statistics-coupled data (Solar, R=0.06); (iii) forecasting and
imputation only.
